### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期 ，維持政策利率不變。 決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂 ， 也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席Kevin Warsh在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在 「通膨高企」 與 「經濟強韌」 雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下 ，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變 。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息 ， 為歐元

提供部分支撐 ， 但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國 ， 在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下 ， 歐元反彈力道相對有限 。 目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善 ， 但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪； 周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變 ，美元指數自 102
上方回落 ， 加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐， 境外人民幣突破6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升 。 就中國國內市場來看 ，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔 ， 美中利差維持在相對高位 ，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度 ；
最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散 ，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號， USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：156.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.63 158.34

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息 ， 升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢 。二 、 歐元區第三季GDP
年增 1.3%， 高於市場預期的1.1%， 顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下 ，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場 。 此外 ，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%， 優於市場預期的3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下 ， 市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩 ，預計將限縮歐元走升空間 。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破 ， 顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上 ， 預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1050-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1103 1.1226

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.27-115.10.30 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109R 2.03% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.845%~1.846%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 時序邁入月底 ， 銀行間已大致調整好積數 ， 且央行未明顯
增發定存單，令市場資金充裕。近兩日股市回落，令週五資金需求上揚，惟供
需尚屬平衡，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.825% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週台債市場交投依舊清淡 ，10 年期指標券 A15109R 等殖系統成交在 2.03%，
5 年期指標券 A15108 議價成交在 1.845%~1.846%。 
美債利率自波段高點回落，且央行總裁楊金龍本週立法院發言維持近來對通
膨容忍度變寬之態度 ， 惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除 ， 買盤追價動力有
限，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，關注下週30 年期公債標售果，預估 5 年
券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

## Document 090: 2327國巨 宏遠

Source PDF: all report pdf/2327國巨 宏遠.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
2327 國巨(Yageo)

報告日期： 2026/10/30 研 究 員：洪瑞陽  (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 既有調價與 AI 產品出貨增加，帶動 3Q26 毛利率升至 41.0% 、
EPS 達 5.83 元；第四季雖為消費性淡季，公司仍指引營收、
毛利率及營益率小幅季增，呈現淡季不淡。  
 3Q26 AI 相關應用占營收 19.8% ，較前季 16% 提高 3.8 個百分
點， MLCC 、鉭電容與電源端產品共同帶動成長。第三季訂單
出貨比仍接近 2，公司規劃 2027 年 MLCC 及鉭電容擴產 10%
至 15% ，自第二季及下半年分批開出。  
 預估 2026 ／2027 年營收 1,841.63 ／2,498.39 億元 ，YoY 增長
38.5% ／35.7% ，EPS 為 20.68 ／35.71 元。 2027 年獲利成長
來自既有調價全年化、稼動率升至滿載、擴產及高階產品組合
提升。

一、 AI 需求帶動，第四季營收與獲利率續增  
3Q26 營收 495.32 億元， QoQ 增長 11.4% 、YoY 增長 49.7% ，連續
第七季季增，創下單季營收歷史新高。價格調整逐步反映，加上
AI 產品組合與營運效率改善，毛利率達 41.0% ，較前季提高 2.5
個百分點；營業利益率 29.5% ，EPS 為 5.83 元。投入成本提高抵
銷部分效益，但營收與毛利成長仍帶動獲利增加。

AI 相關應用占營收已由 1Q26 的 15%、2Q26 的 16%， 提高至3Q26
的 19.8% 。按公布占比計算，第三季 AI 相關營收約 98.07 億元，
較第二季約 71.13 億元增長 37.9% 。成長涵蓋 MLCC 、鉭電容，以
及電源端使用的薄膜與鋁電容，反映運算、通訊與供電需求共同
增加。工業營收亦已連續七季成長，車用維持正向，消費性營收
仍有成長，但速度低於其他應用。

第四季通常為消費性產品淡季，公司考量接單及在手訂單仍強，
指引營收、毛利率與營業利益率均較第三季小幅提高。本研究採
稼動率約 95% ，以既有調價遞延反映支撐淡季營收，預估 4Q26
營收 520.09 億元， QoQ 增長 5.0% 、YoY 增長 44.6% ，毛利率
42.78% 、營業利益率 31.43% 、EPS 為 6.36 元。

二、稼動率接近滿載，新增產能 2Q27 起貢獻  
3Q26 標準型與利基型產品稼動率均接近 95% ，公司預期 4Q26 超
過 95% ，並透過去瓶頸、效率改善與逐步擴充產能支應訂單。第

三季營收已較前季增加 11.4% ，訂單出貨比仍接近 2，顯示較高出
貨基期下接單維持高水準 ， 尤其MLCC 與鉭電容等 AI 相關產品需
求較強。

公司規劃 2027 年 MLCC 及鉭電容產能較目前增加 10% 至 15% ，
部分於第二季、部分於下半年開出。此為相對目前產能的整體增
幅，新增產能須隨設備交付、驗證與量產進度反映；公司亦表示，
擴產後希望稼動率回到 85% 至 90% 的健康水準。

三、漲價分批反映， 2027 年獲利仍以電容產品為主  
國巨 2026 年 2 月調高晶片電阻價格 10% 至 20%， 並同步調整鉭電
容； 7 月調價範圍擴至各產品線，假設幅度約 15% 至 25% ，並涵
蓋 EMS 與 OEM 直接客戶，通常延後一至兩季反映，因此第三季
已認列部分效益， 10 月新一季價格生效。

MLCC 與鉭電容為目前成長最明確的產品。第三季 MLCC 占營收
21.5% ，高於前季 19.1% ；鉭電容占 23.7% ，營收季增 10.0% 、年
增 45.3%。 公司表示鉭粉價格自年初趨於穩定 ， 已進行策略採購及
長期供應鏈布局，支持後續供貨與成本管理。

四、預估 2026 ／2027 年 EPS 為 20.68 ／35.71 元 
預估 2026 ／2027 年營收 1,841.63 ／2,498.39 億元 ，YoY 增長 38.5%
／35.7% ，EPS 為 20.68 ／35.71 元。2027 年隨既有調價全年反映、
假設稼動率提高至 100%，以 及MLCC 與鉭電容新增產能自第二季
起分批貢獻，帶動營收與獲利成長。

五、投資建議：買進，目標價 892 元 
參考國外 MLCC 大廠本益比，以及國巨 AI 佔比持續提高，給予
25 倍本益比，以 2027 年 EPS 計算 35.71 ，目標價 892 元，投資建
議為買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、完整被動元件產品線， AI 相關由板端延伸至電源  
國巨透過產品開發及 Pulse 、KEMET 、奇力新等併購布局，產品涵
蓋 MLCC 、鉭電容、鋁電容、薄膜電容、電阻、磁性元件與感測
器，服務工業、運算、通訊、車用及消費性市場。 AI 設備從機房
供電、機櫃電源轉換到運算板，都需要處理電壓穩定、濾波、儲
能及訊號干擾；不同位置的電壓、空間與可靠度要求不同，使完
整產品組合有利於參與客戶多個供電環節。

3 
公司第三季 AI 相關應用占比接近兩成，已分布於多項產品線。隨
運算功耗提高，成長範圍由靠近 GPU 與 ASIC 的板端電容，擴大
至通訊設備、電源模組及機櫃供電。磁性元件可參與電源轉換及
濾波，電阻用於訊號與電流相關功能，感測產品則提供溫度等監
測需求。

供應能力也逐漸成為競爭條件。國巨的多產品、全球製造據點及
既有客戶關係，有助協調產品開發與後續供貨； TDK 與太陽誘電
討論先進 MLCC 、電感的共同開發及製造、採購合作，亦反映高
容值、高可靠度與穩定供應的重要性提高。

二、MLCC 與鉭電容為主軸，高容值與穩定性優勢擴大  
AI 運算功耗與供電密度增加，要求電容在有限空間內提供足夠電
容量，並維持供電穩定。 MLCC 具備小型化與高密度配置的優勢，
對高容值、小尺寸與可靠度的要求隨平台升級提高；鉭電容則具
備高容值與電容量穩定性優勢，可配合運算及通訊設備的不同低
壓電源設計，支應儲能與濾波需求。兩者實際搭配取決於電路位
置、電壓、體積與成本。

國巨透過 KEMET 具備鉭電容與多類電容產品布局，可配合客戶
選擇不同供電方案。當高容值 MLCC 供應吃緊或設計成本提高
時，其他電容可提供設計選項，但轉換仍須完成電路調整與驗證，
不能將短缺直接換算成鉭電容新增訂單。本研究以目前已觀察到
的產品成長、擴產與價格假設推估， 2027 年 MLCC 及鉭電容營收
分別為 628.36 億元及 669.79 億元， YoY 增長 65.6% 及 51.1% ，合
計營收占比由 2026 年的 44.67% 升至 51.96% 。

電源端亦提供後續產品升級機會。隨資料中心朝更高功率及 800V
直流供電方向發展，不同轉換位置將提高耐壓、溫度穩定性及效
率要求。高壓端與轉換後低壓端所需元件不同，國巨可透過陶瓷、
薄膜、鋁電容與磁性元件參與多段供電。

三、日韓接單與交期延長，高階電容供需偏緊延續  
日韓大廠的出貨與接單，顯示高階電容需求已反映在營運數據。
依， JEITA 公布最新 2026 年 7 月日廠被動元件全球出貨額 4,280
億日圓，年增 23.2% ；村田因 AI 伺服器用需求急速成長，村田製
作所將擴建 MLCC 工廠，滿足需求增加。村田之前 4 月宣布，計
畫在截至 2028 年 3 月為止的 2 年 期 間，對MLCC 生產設備投資約
800 億日圓，提升 10-15% 產能。關於投資計畫，這次中島規巨表
示，「正評估能將投資時程提前多少」 。表示「原先預估位於島根
縣出雲市的工廠產能要到 2030 年才會滿載，如今卻可能提前至
2026 年就達到滿載」

根據 Future Electronics 最新資料亦顯示交期延長 。 村田低於1μF
產品交期由 9 月 26–28 週延長至 10 月 30–36 週，高於 1μF 及
車規產品由 26–30 週延長至 30–36 週； TDK 低於 1μF 產品由
26–36 週延長至 30–40 週，高 於1μF 及車規產品由 26–48 週調
整至 30–48 週。三星電機上述三類產品均由 30 週以上調整至 52
週，價格維持向上。國巨低於 1μF、高於 1μF 及車規產品交期
均維持 30–52 週，交期與價格趨勢持續向上，整體而言， AI 伺服
器與電源規格升級持續帶動被動元件需求，交期不僅維持高檔，
主要 MLCC 廠及部分電阻產品更進一步拉長，漲價品項亦有增
加，延續供不應求的正向看法。

5 
高效能晶片的 AI 伺服器中不可或缺的元件。隨著大型科技公司準
備明年推出下一代 AI 伺服器，三星電機預計 AI 用 MLCC 的需求
將達到今年的約八倍 ， 這項預測較上一季公佈的超七倍有所上調。

國巨本次法說表示，全球代理商庫存在過去三個月由約五個月降
至四個月，大中華區代理商維持兩個月以下。供應商接單偏強、
交期延長與通路庫存下降相互呼應，增加客戶提前確保供應的需
求。

四、滿載提高既有產出， 2027 年擴產分批支應訂單

本研究預期 2027 年高階電容供需仍偏緊，主要考量需求成長、產
品升級與新增供給之間存在時間差。 AI 平台提升功耗與供電密
度，對電容量及小型化提出更高要求；高階產品也會改變產能配
置，設備投入增加未必等比例轉化為可交付的標準品顆數。三星
規劃菲律賓新廠於 2027 年量產高附加價值 MLCC，同時仍預期高
階供應偏緊，村田將兩年擴產 10-15% 計畫考慮提前，皆是供不應
求現象。

國巨新增產能自第二季及下半年陸續開出，第一季主要仍依靠既
有產能與去瓶頸。本研究採 MLCC 、鉭電容年底有效產能較目前
增加 15% ，全年平均增幅為 7.5% 。因此，年底產能增加 15% 不等
於全年供給增加 15%； 加上第三季訂單出貨比仍接近 2， 新增供給
需先支應既有接單與後續需求持續。

基於交期延長、高階產品需求增加及擴產分批到位，本研究假設
國巨於 2027 年針對 MLCC 及鉭電容有機會再調價 ， 根據今年國巨
2026 年 2 月調高晶片電阻價格 10% 至 20% ，並同步調整鉭電容；
7 月調價範圍擴至各產品線，假設幅度約 15% 至 25% ，先假設明
年特定產品再調升 15% ，並假設於下半年完整反應。此輪假設調
價僅涵蓋兩項電容，屬同規格售價提高，與既有升規組合及 2026
年調價全年化分開計算 。(註：15% 幅度及生效時點為本研究假設，
並非公司已公布指引。 )

成本方面，公司表示鉭粉價格已趨穩定，且已進行策略採購，支
持供貨及成本管理。

二、預估 2027 年營收 2,498.39 億元， EPS 為 35.71 元 
2027 年各季維持 100% 稼動率 ，MLCC 與鉭電容有效產能自第二季
起逐步增加 ， 並沿用高階產品組合提升的假設 。 第一季營收562.55
億元， QoQ 增長 8.2% ，反映稼動率由 95% 升至 100% 、調價剩餘
效益及產品組合提升。另假設第二季僅 MLCC 及鉭電容再調價
15%，第三季起完整反映，第四季維持新價格，貢獻全年新增營收
96.39 億元。第二至第四季營收分別為 587.39 、659.50 、688.95 億
元，全 年 營 收2,498.39 億元 ，YoY 增長 35.7%。 預估毛利率47.09%、
營業費用 247 億元、費用率 9.89% ，營業利益 929.55 億元， YoY
增長 77.7% ；業外淨收入 18.60 億元、基本稅率 22% ，全年 EPS
為 35.71 元， YoY 增長 72.7% 。

本次報告：法說會

國巨* (2327 TT) 
淡季不淡，第四季續強

永豐觀點 
AI 相關占比達 20%，較上季提升 4%。稼動率方面，將較上季 95%持續成長，拋
開過往明顯淡旺季，營運成長可期。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進投資建議：主係看好國巨營運將受惠於(1) 日韓廠商將低階 MLCC 產能
移轉至高階產品的訂單外溢效應，(2) 預估 4Q26 稼動率逾 95%。另一方面，漲價
效應浮現將進一步推升毛利率表現， (3) AI 伺服器電源規格提升下，國巨在 AI 
Server 產品線齊全，除 MLCC 外，鉭質電容、電感及晶片電阻的用量增加。綜上
所述，國巨受惠轉單效應、被動元件產業循環向上、仍有持續漲價機會，故維持買
進的投資建議，以 2027-2028 年平均 EPS 預估，給予 27 倍本益比，目標價 940
元。

ESG 評析 
國巨企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

### Page 2

2

國巨集團為台灣被動元件第一大供應商：3Q26 營收產品別占比 MLCC 21.5%，
Magnetics 22.3%，T antalum(Kemet) 23.7%，Resistor 15.3%，Sensor 10.6%，
Other Components 6.6%，其中 MLCC 與電阻成長主要來自標準品與特殊品需求
強勁 ； 終端應用占比 ：Industrial 28.7%，Computing & Enterprise System 28.3%，
Automotive 14.9%，Consumer 11.7%，T elecom 12.5%，Aerospace/Defense 
/Medical 3.9%，其中 AI 佔比約為 20%，較上季增加 4%。 
價格調漲及稼動率提升，3Q26 獲利優於預期：受惠於 AI 相關及工業 Industrial
需求強勁，帶動各產品線、應用市場營收成長，其中標準品及特殊品皆成長，AI 相
關營收比重達 19.8%。國巨 3Q26 營收 459.32 億元(+11.4% QoQ，+49.7% YoY)，
受惠產品組合及價格調整，毛利率 41%(+2.5pts QoQ，+4.8pts YoY)，優於預期
的 39.5%，營利率 29.5%(+3.1ptsQoQ，+6.6ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 119.8 億元
(+27.2%QoQ，+88.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 5.83 元，優於預期的 5.27 元。 
動能延續，4Q26 規模經濟效應顯現：展望第四季，國巨的 B/B Ratio 約為 2，仍
處於歷史高檔水準。稼動率方面，預將優於上季的 95%。在稼動率持續提升且先
前與客戶的漲價協議陸續發酵下 ， 研究部預估國巨4Q26 營收 532.44 億元(+7.5% 
QoQ，+48% YoY)，受惠產品組合及價格調整，毛利率 41.5%(+0.5pts QoQ ，
+4.2pts YoY)，營利 29.8%(+0.3ptsQoQ，+5.5ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 132.39 億元
(+10.5%QoQ，+96.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 6.45 元 。 預估國巨2026 年營收 1853.98
億（+39.5%YoY） ，毛利率40%(+3.8pts YoY)，營利率28%(+5.6pts YoY)，稅後淨
利 426.4 億元(+80.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 20.76 元。 
產業循環向上空間可期：在 AI 伺服器電源規格不斷提升下，被動元件用量明顯增
加，日韓廠商皆發出漲價通知，國巨受惠於高階 MLCC 產品用量的增加外，鋁質
及鉭質電容等用量增加顯著。此外，預期在轉單效應顯現後，將使國巨在中低階產
品具有市場主導權。若以應用別區分，目前以 AI 相關終端需求最為強勁，國巨產
品亦將受惠鉭質電容 、 電感及晶片電阻的用量增加 ； 車用亦有復甦 。 展望2027 年，
國巨將於 2Q27 陸續開出新產能，預估總產能將年增 10-15%。此外，除 AI 相關
需求持續暢旺外，因競爭對手產能調整，產生的產能缺口難在短期補足，轉單效應
將至少延續至 2Q27，被動元件產業循環仍在持續向上，預期2027 年 MLCC 仍有
30%以上的漲價空間，其餘被動元件也有望因產能排擠使供給出現缺口，帶動下波
漲價 ， 並透過與客戶簽訂長期合約確保營運延續性 ， 故預估國巨2027 年營收 2422.2
億（+30.6%YoY），毛利率43.6%(+3.6pts YoY)，營利率 32%(+4pts YoY)，稅後

## Document 127: 261029_gs_EMC

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

We hosted EMC’s 3Q26 post result call on October 29th. Key 
takeaways include: 
(1) EMC mentioned its proactive capacity expansion plans in coming 
years (capacity will go up to 15.25mn sheets/month by end of 2030 
vs. 5.85mn sheets/month now) are still not enough to support all the 
LTA they signed for the next 4 years, implying a solid demand 
outlook without much risk on oversupply.  
(2) In terms of the CCL pricing, the company mentioned it’s doing 
the second round of the high-end CCL (M7+) pricing hike this year, 
with 5-20% pricing hike ratio, which not only on M7/M8, but also on 
M8+/M9 grade CCL.  
(3) For 4Q26, EMC mentioned the conservative estimate on revenue 
growth rate should be at least 15-20% QoQ, with GM could at least 
expand to 38.0%-39.5% (note that the company estimated 3Q rev 
could grow 15% QoQ and GM could be 33.5%-36.5% back to 2Q26 
result call - here, but the actual outcome was revenue up 29% QoQ 
and GM hit 37.7%).  
(4) For substrate material, the company mentioned it is focusing on 
the high-end ABF substrate core-layer CCL (BT type, but for ABF 
substrate), and will hold 50% market share for the MTK TPU v8 ABF 
substrate. EMC also mentioned that it is now working with several 
customers on high end ABF used CCL qualification projects which 
the company expects to start shipment from 4Q27. 
(5) For the substrate material capacity, EMC now holds 300k 
sheets/month capacity, but will expand the capacity to at least 
1.2mn sheets/month by end of 2027. EMC mentioned the proactive 
expansion is their strategy on always prepare capacity ahead of 
customers’ demand, and mentioned these new capacity can also be 
used to produce high-end M9/M10/M11 CCL if the demand cannot 
fill all the capacity.

### Page 3

(6) In terms of the profitability of the substrate material, the company mentioned the
GM of this product will be at least 50%+, despite its relatively competitive pricing
compares to the market leader who has 65%+ GM on substrate material.
(7) EMC mentioned it does see AI customers start qualifying second or third sources to
improve the stability of the supply chain, which should give more room for competitors
to enter the market. However, the company continues to target 50% AI CCL market

share in the long term, and believes its best in class product quality and deep
cooperation with PCB/CSP customers can see it continue to hold the leadership position
in this high-end industry.
Investment view - read across for high-end CCL players
We maintain our positive view on the high-end CCL players, including EMC and TUC,
while we see both companies are enjoying not only favorable pricing conditions on
high-end CCL (due to the wide supply demand gap) but also the accelerating product

mix improvement speed (we expect M9 massive adoption to start from 2H27 with 50%+
adoption rate for next generation AI server, and expect EMC to start M10 CCL product
shipment from 2H27 with mass volume shipment to start from 2028). See our Oct 28
report.
Both EMC and TUC mentioned the proactive capacity expansion they are planning in the
next 3-5 years (both companies will have 150-250% more capacity in 2029-2030 vs.
today) are still not enough to support the high-end AI/LEO/switching customers’

demand, suggesting an even more favorable pricing outlook. We expect the high-end
CCL pricing will go up by 40%+ YoY in 2027 considering the increasing tightness
condition of the high-end CCL industry (vs. 20-30% YoY 2026), which should drive both
company’s GM further in coming years.
In terms of competition, while we already model EMC’s AI CCL market share to decease
from 60-70% in 2026 to ~50% in the next three years, we do believe EMC’s earnings will

grow by 90% 2026-29 CAGR even with this 50% market share. For TUC, we expect it
should be the key share gainer from EMC considering CSPs duo source policy (we think
TUC would be a strong option due to not only its better technology skillset but also its
proactive expansion plans while other high-end CCL players are not expanding capacity
or only expanding slightly on the capacity), and we expect the company will have
multiple AI customers including AI ASIC/GPU end customers from 2H27.

Overall, we are even more positive on EMC/TUC compared to 3 months ago, after seeing
customers solid plans on high-end CCL LTA and customers’ design upgrade on CCL spec
(we had expected M10 MP would happen in 2029 or beyond only). We believe
EMC/TUC’s key competition advantage is not only from the leadership position in the
high-end CCL market, but also their proactive capacity expansion plans which makes
them attractive to AI customers as most of the other high-end CCL players are not

expanding enough capacity in 2027/28.
Maintain Buy on EMC / TUC with TP of NT$11,700 / NT$3,460, implying 134%/150%
upside vs. last close.

### Page 5

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Elite Material  
EMC, a key supplier of high-end HDI material (70%+ market share) and SLP material 
(90%+ market share), has been focusing on the high-speed switch/server CCL market for 
3-5+ years. We are positive on its market share trajectory in the server industry and 
expect EMC to increase its share from <10% in the Purley generation to 15-20%/20+% 
in the Whitley/Eagle Stream platforms. EMC is also proactively expanding its share in the 
switch market (40%/30%+ in 2024/23 vs. <5% before 2019), with an expanding AI 
customer base (only one 400G customer in 3Q20, to being one of the largest suppliers in 
2023), which should continue to benefit the company’s top/bottom line growth over 
time. We believe EMC’s leading position in the AI server CCL market should drive strong 
revenue growth and GM/OPM expansion in the long term, given that it is the major 
supplier for all Nvidia/Google/AWS AI server projects, and we expect it to maintain its 
leadership position. Considering its leading position in the high-end CCL industry, solid 
production skills and earnings growth outlook, we are Buy rated on the stock. With the 
shares trading at a 2026E P/E below AI server component suppliers and supported by a 
solid growth outlook, we view valuation as attractive. 
Key downside risks include: (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) 
rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and 
(3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Elite Material  
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$11,700 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E 
(in line with the peak P/E multiple for AI component suppliers in the past 3 years).  
Key downside risks:  (1) RCC to replace all high-end smartphone HDI design; (2) rising 
trade tensions, which could lead to weaker smartphone and server shipments; and (3) 
rising competition from mainland China peers. 
Investment Thesis - Taiwan Union Technology Corp. 
TUC is a key high-end global CCL supplier that focuses on M7+ grade high-speed CCL 
(key applications include high-end switches (100G+) and AI server materials), with 20%+ 
market share in the past two years. The company continues to expect its unit market 
share in the server industry to increase from 15% in Whitley and Purley processors to 
higher in the Eagle Stream generation, and plans to launch two types of products for 
low-end and high-end customers (T2A and T2C) to gain market share. Also, strong 
growth in 400G/800G switch shipments and new 800G switch shipments, scheduled for 
launch in 2025-27, should continue to be a key demand driver for TUC, in our view. 
Additionally, the company is working on high-end AI projects with CSP and enterprise 
players, which we believe should drive revenue momentum in the long term. We view the 
stock as undervalued vs. Taiwan CCL peers on a P/E basis and rate it Buy.  
Key downside risks include: (1) slower-than-expected share gains in the low-loss CCL 
segment; (2) rising trade tensions, which could lead to weaker server and switch 
shipments globally; and (3) rising competition from mainland China peers.

### Page 3

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Structural OPM upward trend — Management expects 4Q26 to mark the 
beginning of a structural improvement in OPM, supported by rapidly scaling data 
center ASIC revenue and operating leverage. Management indicated that the OPM 
could exceed 20% in 2027. Meanwhile, selective pricing adjustments across 
traditional businesses should help offset rising supply chain costs. Beyond data 
centers, Smart Edge should benefit from new AI PCs, AI tablets, automotive 
platforms and networking applications, including continued collaboration with 
NVIDIA on next-generation AI computing products. While smartphone demand 
remains relatively subdued, management expects the expanding AI business to 
increasingly dominate MediaTek’s overall growth and earnings trajectory.
Second-Generation ASIC on Track for 1Q28 
Production, with a Second Customer 
Management expressed increasing confidence in achieving the upper end of its 15–
20% market share target for the estimated US$80bn data center AI accelerator 
ASIC market in 2027, implying approximately US$16bn in potential revenue. This 
represents a substantial increase from the revised US$2.5bn target for 2026, 
supported by strong demand visibility and continued supply chain expansion. 
Management emphasized that securing adequate foundry, packaging and other 
critical component capacity remains essential to meeting customer requirements. 
Importantly, the revenue opportunity reflects MediaT ek’s increasing participation in 
the value chain, encompassing advanced process technologies, packaging, high-
speed interconnects and custom silicon integration. Despite recent market 
concerns over AI infrastructure financing and CSP capital expenditure 
sustainability, management remains confident that AI compute demand will 
continue growing over the next several years, particularly for customized 
accelerators offering superior performance per watt and total cost of ownership.
Figure 1. Mediatek - 3Q26 results overview 
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Management reiterated that its second AI accelerator project remains on schedule 
for high-volume production in 1Q28, despite market concerns regarding design 
complexity, advanced packaging yields and potential delays. The new accelerator is 
expected to deliver multiple times the performance per watt of the first-generation 
product, significantly improving customer TCO and strengthening Media T ek’s 
competitive positioning . Management acknowledged manufacturing challenges 
associated with its U.S. IDM packaging partner but highlighted progress in 
identifying key yield constraints and implementing corrective measures, expressing 
high confidence in further yield improvements. Importantly, Media T ek also 
confirmed a separate design win with a second, non-Chinese data center

Prepared for Kevin Lu

### Page 4

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
4
customer, with initial revenue expected in 2028. The company is already
collaborating with its existing customer on subsequent-generation products,
although specifications are still being finalized. Management expects another year
of strong revenue growth in 2028 but declined to provide specific revenue guidance
or customer contribution estimates.
Expanding competitive advantages through advanced node

design/packaging, SerDes and NVIDIA NVLink Fusion
MediaT ek continues investing in advanced technology capabilities, including 448G
SerDes, advanced packaging, HBM integration and co-packaged optics (CPO), to
establish a competitive advantage in increasingly complex AI infrastructure
designs. Management emphasized that its differentiation comes not from
individual technologies but from integrating them into high-performance, power-
efficient and commercially viable solutions. More importantly, its partnership with

NVIDIA through NVLink Fusion enables customers to integrate custom XPUs into
NVIDIA’s rack-scale ecosystem, while its support for UALink and Ethernet-based
architectures provides greater deployment flexibility. MediaT ek is expanding
beyond individual silicon components toward subsystems and rack-level
solutions, shortening customers’ development cycles and accelerating time to
revenue. Management indicated encouraging engagement with multiple potential

customers and expects broader adoption opportunities. The 448G SerDes
development is progressing well and is expected to support both existing
accelerator programs and future customers, with management not anticipating it
to become a critical schedule bottleneck.
Structural OPM expansion trend; Reiterate Buy
Management expects 4Q26 to mark the beginning of a structural improvement in
operating profitability, supported by rapidly scaling data center ASIC revenue and

operating leverage. Although ASIC gross margins are expected to remain below
MediaT ek’s traditional corporate average, the significantly larger revenue
contribution and more favorable operating expense leverage should drive
meaningful operating margin expansion. Management indicated that the operating
margin could exceed 20% in 2027, potentially somewhat higher, compared with its
historical mid-teen level. Meanwhile, selective pricing adjustments across

traditional businesses should help offset rising supply chain costs, although these
increases are primarily intended to preserve profitability rather than expand gross
margins. Beyond data centers, Smart Edge should benefit from new AI PCs, AI
tablets, automotive platforms and networking applications, including continued
collaboration with NVIDIA on next-generation AI computing products. While
smartphone demand remains relatively subdued, management expects the

expanding AI business to increasingly dominate MediaT ek’s overall growth and
earnings trajectory.
Given MediaT ek’s better operating leverage, we lift our earnings projection for
2027 /2028 5%/5%, mainly reflecting better OPM. We maintain our Buy rating with
an unchanged TP of NT$6,800, implying 29x our 2027 /28 EPS estimates.

### Page 9

Mediatek (2454.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
9
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 29x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered

stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also

suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in

exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility

or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Verena Jeng 
+852-2978-1681 | verena.jeng@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. 
Allen Chang 
+852-2978-2930 | 
allen.k.chang@gs.com

VPEC (2455.TW): Optoelectronics mix upgrade driving GM expansion; 
3Q26 in line; Buy
2 November 2026 | 5:30AM HKT
3Q26 revenues increased 39% QoQ, with optoelectronics (mainly InP epiwafer for PD 
and CW lasers) revenues contribution up to 43% (vs. 27% in 2Q26), driving GM up to 
45% (vs. 39% in 2Q26). Along with opex ratio further improved to 13.7% (vs. 15.4% 
in 2Q26), VPEC’s OP income increased 87% QoQ in 3Q26, or 2% behind / 7% above 
our / Bloomberg consensus estimates. Looking ahead, we expect 4Q26E revenues to 
further increase by 14% QoQ, with GM up to 46% driven by product mix upgrade 
toward Optoelectronics. The company also announced its issuance of unsecured 
convertible bonds valued at NT$1.5bn (US$46m), to supplement its procurement in 
raw materials and equipment, reflecting the strong demand and VPEC’s commitment 
in capacity expansion; as we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates) where 
our US Internet team forecast that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in 
aggregate would grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026E-28E 
and where we raise our estimates by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E. We remain positive view on VPEC, which we believe is positioned to benefit 
from the AI server racks ramp up, optical networking specification upgrades across 
transmitters / CW lasers (toward 1.6T optical modules) and receivers (toward 200G 
PD), product expansion toward EML base epiwafers, further customer penetration 
for CW lasers, improving InP substrate supply, and opportunities in InP epiwafers for 
high power lasers for NPO and CPO. Maintain Buy.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price from 2029E P/E discounted back 
to 2027E at 10.5% COE (no change), and derive our target P/E multiple from refreshed 
peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year fundamentals (NI YoY and OPM). Based on our 
2029E-30E NI YoY of 18% (unchanged) and 2029-30E OPM of 38% (unchanged), and 
peers’ PEG&M of 0.4x (unchanged), we derive target P/E multiple at 22.0x (vs. 22.2x 
previously), reflecting a slight peer group multiple compression amid the product 
transition window from 800G to 1.6T optical transceivers, balanced by sustained 
long-term AI optical networking demand. Our new 12-month target price is at NT$785 
(vs. NT$793 previously). Our new TP implies 2027E P/E at 68.7x (vs. 69.4x previously), 
within the company’s trading range (vs. peak at 74x, and +1stv. at 47x). Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

Earnings revision:  
We revise up our 2026 earnings estimates by 1% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise

Valuation:

based on 22.6 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E since 2022, 
and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Fositek is a global leading hinges supplier, mainly for foldable smartphones (56% of 
2025 revenues). We expect the company’s foldable smartphone hinges revenues to 
show solid growth in 2026/27E driven by (1) rising foldable phone market penetration 
rate; (2) expanding customer base, from China smartphones to more global-tier 
smartphones; and (3) stabilized smartphone market demand, encouraging brand makers 
to launch new form factors to attract consumers. Competitive edges of Fositek lie in its 
strong innovation (e.g., hinges for rollable phones, wearable phones), in-house molding 
and automated production to secure precision, long experience to support fast delivery, 
and leading customer base with expansion (e.g., supplying Huawei since the first 
foldable phone back in 2019).  We continue to see Fositek successfully entering the 
AI-related business, especially QD (quick disconnect) products for AI server liquid 
cooling. The company has the largest QD production capacity (4.5mn units/month) 
among Taiwanese thermal suppliers and will continue to expand if demand continues to 
accelerate. Furthermore, the company continues to make progress with its rail kit 
business, which will continue to break into AI servers (including both GPU servers and 
ASIC servers). Overall, we believe Fositek will successfully transition from a 
consumer-focused company to AI-focused company, with server-related business to 
contribute 94% of total revenue in 2028E (vs. 50% in 2025), and will see GM continue to 
expand to 37.2% in 2028E (vs. 26.3% in 2025).  
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Fositek 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$2,390 is based on a target 22.6x 2027 P/E, 
which is the company’s average P/E since 2022. We consider this multiple appropriate 
given the increasing contribution from AI-related products (including QD and rail kits) 
could offset the potential weakness from its foldable smartphone business. 
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected contribution from AI-related products, 
(2) slower-than-expected foldable phone penetration; and (3) Potential competition 
from more suppliers

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

## Document 192: 261105_gs_nypcb

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_gs_nypcb.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Investment Thesis - NYPCB 
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit 
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB 
considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate 
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the 
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the 
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end 
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding 
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s 
high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook 
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger 
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the 
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long 
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s 
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148% 
2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s 
earnings and stock performance.  
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB 
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based 
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).  
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2) 
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3) 
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

## Document 249: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

## Document 257: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡_62bfa2bd

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금두산에너빌리티(034020) 3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡는다면 지금3Q26 Review_ 바닥을 잡_62bfa2bd.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY) 을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%) 이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY) 을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 271: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5.pdf

Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82만원에서 64만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

## Document 284: AI in 2027_ 20 questions for the global economy

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI in 2027_ 20 questions for the global economy.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 24
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The AI boom is reshaping growth, productivity, trade, jobs and
markets. Investment still has room to run in 2027, but
ﬁnancing is getting riskier and expectations are sky-high. We
ask how long it can last, who stands to gain and what happens
if the promised returns fail to arrive
Carsten Brzeski, Bert Colijn, James Knightley, Deepali Bhargava,
James Smith, Lynn Song, Jan Frederik Slijkerman and Amrita Naik
Nimbalkar
In a nutshell
The AI boom still has room to run. Investment is likely to

continue rising in 2027, supported by strong demand
and constrained supply. But its contribution to US growth
is probably close to its peak as the sheer scale of
investment makes further acceleration harder.
The boom is entering a new phase. The story is
expanding beyond chips and data centres into power
generation, grids and cooling. At the same time,
ﬁnancing is shifting gradually from hyperscalers'
enormous cash ﬂows towards debt, private credit and

### Page 2

more complex structures, increasing the risks if expected
returns fail to materialise.
The economic eﬀects are becoming broader and more
complicated. AI is supporting US growth and global
technology supply chains, but may also be diverting
investment from other parts of the economy. It is adding
to inﬂationary pressures today through demand for
energy, equipment and skilled labour, while the hoped-
for productivity and disinﬂation beneﬁts are likely to take

longer to emerge.
The winners will not necessarily be those building the
most AI infrastructure. Europe and Asia are beneﬁting
from critical positions in the supply chain, while
ultimately the largest gains may go to companies and
economies that use AI most eﬀectively.
Our base case is a plateau, not a bust. Today's leading
technology companies are highly proﬁtable and much
less leveraged than their dotcom predecessors. But
expectations are exceptionally high. The crucial question

for 2027 is whether AI revenues and productivity gains
begin to justify the extraordinary investment. If they do
not, overcapacity, tighter ﬁnancing and falling valuations
could turn a plateau into a much harder landing.
How big, how long?
Railroads, electriﬁcation, dotcom ﬁbre: how
does today's AI boom compare with the
great investment manias of the past?
Bigger than the dotcom boom, more important for growth and, so
far, more safely ﬁnanced.
Investment in US data centre structures, computers and software

reached 5.6% of GDP in the second quarter of 2026, up from 3.8% in
2022, or 5.2% once imported hardware is netted out. That already

### Page 3

puts the current boom above the dotcom peak in 2000, when
business investment in computers, communications equipment
and software reached about 4.5% of GDP, although still below
Britain’s railway mania of the 1840s, when railway investment
alone reached roughly 6-7% of GDP.
So while this is not the biggest investment boom in history, it is
already bigger than the last great technology boom. And it is
having a much bigger economic impact: IT investment accounted

for 16-18% of US GDP growth in 1998-2000; AI-related investment
now accounts for 37%.
The biggest diﬀerence though is how this boom is being ﬁnanced.
Railways and telecom networks were built on debt and ended in
defaults, largely through unrated railroad bonds in the 19th century
and high-yield telecom debt after 2000. This time, investment has
so far been funded mainly from the cash ﬂows of some of the
world’s most proﬁtable companies, with debt and equity only now

being added at the margin.
The assets are diﬀerent, too. Rails and ﬁbre lasted for decades. Data
centre shells, grids and power plants will also have long lives, but
GPUs may have an economic life of just three to six years. That
much shorter shelf life is central to the debate over whether today’s
investment will generate suﬃcient returns.
Still, the payoﬀ could come faster. Factories took decades to
redesign their ﬂoors around the electric motor. AI coding tools are

paying oﬀ within months, and adoption is already disrupting
software, accounting, online retail and advertising.
The common thread is that transformative technology does not
necessarily mean good returns for those who ﬁnance the
transformation. The Nasdaq lost almost 80% between 2000 and
2002, yet the ﬁbre laid during the boom went on to underpin the
streaming economy. Investors build the road; others get to drive on
it.
How much more will be invested in AI in

2027, and is the boom still accelerating?

### Page 4

Still accelerating in dollars, but probably peaking in its
contribution to growth.
Momentum remains strong. Taiwan's leading semiconductor
companies report order books that are full on a rolling 18-month
basis, with any new capacity snapped up as soon as it comes
online. Industry forecasts point to tight supply-demand conditions
until 2028. Barring a wave of order cancellations or defaults, that
points to another year of heavy AI investment in 2027, with

hyperscaler capex expected to exceed US$1 trillion, increasingly
geared towards AI infrastructure.
So is the boom peaking? In dollar terms, no. The constraint today is
not appetite but supply: chips, power, permits and skilled
electricians. When the bottleneck is the factory gate rather than the
order book, spending is more likely to be stretched out than cut
back.
But economists should be careful what they wish for. GDP growth
responds to the change in investment, not its level. Investment that

rises 50% a year adds a lot to growth; investment that rises 10%
from a much bigger base adds far less; and investment that merely
holds steady at a record high adds nothing at all. As the base gets
bigger, the arithmetic gets tougher. That is why we expect AI
investment to keep rising in 2027 while its contribution to US
growth should peak.
What would signal a genuine top? Shortening lead times for chips
and equipment, cancelled or postponed data centre projects, capex

guidance that stops being revised up, and a widening gap between
investment and AI revenues.
For now, the bigger risk is not that the money runs out in 2027, but
that it keeps ﬂowing so fast that the questions about returns grow
much louder in 2028.
Chips and data centres are only the ﬁrst
layer. How big is the second wave of

### Page 5

investment in grids, power generation and
cooling?
Bigger, slower and longer-lived than the ﬁrst wave – and
potentially more important for the macro outlook.
As a rule of thumb, US$1bn buys roughly 25MW of fully equipped
AI compute capacity. Not all of the c.US$1 trillion of hyperscaler
capex expected in 2027 will go to AI compute; some will fund
conventional cloud infrastructure. But even if only half does, that
implies more than 12GW of additional power demand – roughly the

output of a dozen large nuclear reactors, needed around the clock.
That is where the second wave comes in: transmission lines,
substations and transformers, gas turbines and renewables,
battery storage and industrial cooling. In the US, grid connection
queues and transformer lead times have already become binding
constraints, and some developers are building their own generation
on site rather than waiting for the grid.
This second wave diﬀers from the ﬁrst in three ways. First, the

assets last for decades rather than years. A GPU may be obsolete
by 2030; a substation built today will still be humming in 2060.
That makes grid investment less vulnerable to a sudden AI
downturn and more like the railway build-out of the 19th century.
Second, it is slower. Permitting, planning and construction take
years, which spreads the investment impulse over time and could
stretch out the cycle. Third, it is more widely shared: grid and power

equipment draws on suppliers in Europe and Asia as well as US
construction and engineering ﬁrms, so the beneﬁts travel further.
There is a catch. Somebody has to pay for the grid, and in
regulated markets that somebody is often the household electricity
customer. Rising power bills are already turning data centres into a
political issue in parts of the US, and they feed straight into the
inﬂation debate.
Chips are the sprint. Power is the marathon – and the marathon

may end up mattering more.

### Page 6

Is this an American party, or does Europe
get an invitation? Who supplies the picks
and shovels?
So far it is very much an American party – but Europe supplies the
catering, and may turn out to be the smarter guest.
The investment gap is stark. US imports of computer equipment
and semiconductors, a rough proxy for AI investment, have risen by
about 258% since 2019, compared with 38% in the UK. Europe's
own build-out is held back by high energy prices, scarce grid

capacity – think of the data centre moratoria in Dublin and
Amsterdam – and fragmented capital markets. The EU's AI
gigafactories initiative is an attempt to catch up.
But Europe sells crucial picks and shovels. ASML expects €44-45bn
of sales in 2026 and says customers keep raising their equipment-
demand forecasts because of the scale and duration of AI
investment. Inﬁneon expects its AI-related revenues to rise from
around €1.5bn in FY2026 to €2.5bn in FY2027. Add electrical and

grid equipment, cables, power generation and cooling, and
Europe's industrial base is well plugged into the American build-
out.
Asia has been one of the biggest beneﬁciaries of the AI boom. The
gains extend well beyond Korea and China, which have beneﬁted
from surging demand for memory chips and advanced technology
hardware. Across ASEAN, economies have also attracted signiﬁcant
FDI into semiconductors, electronics manufacturing, and
supporting supply chains as ﬁrms expand capacity and diversify

production networks.
More importantly, the broad economic gains from AI come from
using it, not building it – and here Europe is surprisingly good.
Uptake is strong, and European SMEs use AI almost as intensively
as large corporates, because the upfront investment is low. Nor
does AI look like a winner-takes-all technology: switching costs are
low and network eﬀects limited. If model providers end up
competing on price, the spoils go to the users – and Europe does

### Page 7

not need to build nearly as many data centres to compete. Late to
the construction site, Europe could still be early to move in.
There is a silver lining, too. Europe is far less exposed if the boom
turns to bust. Its AI capex is smaller and the wealth channel much
weaker: equities make up 13% of UK households' ﬁnancial assets,
versus 44% in the US. Missing the party also means skipping the
hangover.
Which sectors stand to win most, and which
risk being left behind?

The winners sell the infrastructure or use AI to do more; the losers
are those whose business model is the very task AI now performs.
The clearest winners are the suppliers of the build-out:
semiconductors and chip equipment, utilities and grid operators,
electrical equipment, construction, cooling and data centre real
estate. They are already seeing it in their order books.
Software is mixed. AI is a powerful selling point, but it also
threatens the per-seat pricing model: if one employee with an AI

agent does the work of ﬁve, the customer needs fewer licences.
Most at risk are legacy IT services and business process
outsourcing, call centres, parts of professional services, and media
and advertising models that depend on search traﬃc. AI-based
services may also disrupt online retail, including online travel
agencies, as AI assistants increasingly do the browsing and the
booking.
But don't write oﬀ every exposed sector. The Jevons paradox –
cheaper supply raising demand – could favour some of them. Legal

services is a good example: if AI makes contract review or legal
advice much cheaper, demand may rise faster than the cost per
task falls. Growth may also come from unexpected places. In
healthcare, AI-driven drug discovery and diagnostics could open up
new treatments, creating demand rather than merely cutting costs.
Ultimately, productivity gains depend more on the tasks
employees perform than on the sector they sit in. Workers handling

potential beneﬁts of generative AI across tasks relate to the degree
of digitalisation across sectors: the more digital a sector already is,
the faster AI can travel through it.
In short, the winners will be those that build AI or use it to work
smarter. The losers will be those whose core activities can
increasingly be automated.

Growth, prices and trade
How much of US growth in 2027 will come
from AI capex, and what would growth look
like without it?
Today, more than a third. In 2027, probably less – which is exactly
why the rest of the economy matters.
At ﬁrst glance, AI looks like the engine of the entire US economy.
Data centre construction, equipment investment and software
spending have grown at a pace that makes their headline
contribution to GDP growth look spectacular. But much of that

money leaves the country straight away: from Taiwanese chips to
servers assembled abroad, the hardware is largely imported.
Netting out those imports, we estimate that AI-related investment
accounts for 37% of US real GDP growth. Substantial, but a long
way from the 'AI is the only game in town' narrative.
The more uncomfortable ﬁnding lies outside tech. Rather than
lifting the overall growth rate, the AI boom seems to have
cannibalised growth elsewhere. Strip out AI, and the rest of US

private non-residential investment had fallen year-on-year for six
consecutive quarters by mid-2026. Before 2021, tech and non-tech
investment moved broadly in step; since then, they have moved in
opposite directions. One explanation: shareholders reward
companies that embrace AI, so capital budgets are being redirected
towards tech at the expense of everything else. So the boom isn't

### Page 9

so much about creating additional investment as redirecting
investment from elsewhere in the economy.
What would US growth look like without AI? Not simply 37% lower.
Part of the capital, engineers and electricity absorbed by AI would
have found other uses. A world without the AI boom would most
likely show weaker growth, but not by the full amount of AI's
contribution – and with a more balanced investment mix.
For 2027, our plateau base case implies a smaller AI contribution to

growth. That would give the non-tech economy room to breathe –
but only if it is ready to take over.
For now, AI is a capex story, not a consumer story. And capex
stories have a habit of ending with a hangover.
The productivity question: when, if ever,
does AI show up in the statistics?
In the US and China, it already does – partly. The big gains are still
to come, and may arrive abruptly.
It depends on where you look. In the US, labour productivity has

picked up markedly since the start of the AI boom. Part of that
comes from high-skilled services, exactly where you would expect
AI to show up ﬁrst. But part of it reﬂects the enormous data centre
investments, which are capital-heavy and need very little labour.
Total factor productivity – the best available measure of gains from
technological progress – has improved decently in the US, but
without an exceptional acceleration. While AI-related sectors are

providing a signiﬁcant boost to several Asian economies, this
largely reﬂects rapid investment and higher capital utilisation
rather than a sustained improvement in underlying productivity.
However, in China, we’ve seen labour productivity growth pick up in
the last two years, breaking what had been an extended downtrend
as Chinese companies race to incorporate AI in workﬂows. In the
eurozone, which lacks comparable AI investment, labour
productivity growth remains lacklustre, with very few exceptions,

like for example, in the Netherlands.

### Page 10

The technology holds real promise for broad-based productivity
gains. But reaping them requires wider adoption and investment in
infrastructure and skills, both within companies and across the
economy. That suggests a J-curve: heavy upfront intangible
investment – time spent learning new skills and reorganising
workﬂows – could initially mute measured productivity before the
investment pays oﬀ, possibly quite abruptly. Electriﬁcation is the
classic precedent: the electric motor arrived in the 1880s, but

factory productivity only took oﬀ in the 1920s, once factories had
been rebuilt around it.
And even then, a shift of the workforce towards sectors where
productivity gains are harder to achieve – think healthcare and care
work – would dampen the macroeconomic eﬀect. Measurement
doesn't help either: when AI makes a free digital service better, GDP
statistics barely notice.
Still, after decades of slowing productivity growth, AI is likely to be a
game changer. The question is not so much whether it will show up

in the statistics, but when. In 1987, Robert Solow quipped that you
could see the computer age everywhere but in the productivity
statistics. It took about a decade to prove him wrong.
Is AI inﬂationary or deﬂationary?
Electricity, memory chips and construction
costs point one way; long-run efﬁciency
gains point the other.
Inﬂationary today, disinﬂationary tomorrow – but don't hold your
breath for the outright deﬂation Silicon Valley promises.
It depends on your time horizon. For now, the eﬀect runs in the

inﬂationary direction. AI's enormous energy demand is adding to
the current energy shock, memory chip prices have surged, and the
scramble for electricians, transformers and construction capacity is
pushing up costs. Meanwhile, the investment boom is pouring fuel
on the ﬁre of a US economy already running with above-target
inﬂation.

### Page 11

In the longer run, once the technology lifts productivity, it should
lower unit labour costs and exert disinﬂationary pressure across the
economy. Like most technological breakthroughs, AI will probably
end up being disinﬂationary. But the eﬀect will be concentrated in
services and much smaller for goods with physical inputs.
Algorithms can't make steel, copper or concrete cheaper.
There is also a less comfortable twist. AI allows companies to price

dynamically, adjusting prices to what each customer is willing to
pay and thereby capturing more of the consumer surplus. That
could make some prices stickier rather than lower. Together with
the physical constraints, this makes the outright deﬂation Sam
Altman envisages rather unlikely.
For AI itself, the picture is diﬀerent. As we argued in 2024, we expect
a downward price trend for AI services, as with other software
categories, and at the sector level we continue to hold that view.

Competition between model providers should keep the pressure on.
For central banks, this means the AI disinﬂation dividend is a story
for the second half of the decade, not for 2027. Until then, the
boom adds to demand faster than it adds to supply – the textbook
recipe for inﬂation, not deﬂation.
How is AI reshaping global trade and
investment ﬂows, from Taiwanese chips to
Dutch lithography?
AI is redrawing global trade around a single supply chain – and

rewarding the countries that own its choke points.
AI is increasingly sorting the global economy into winners and
laggards. The IMF has raised its outlook for economies beneﬁting
from the AI investment boom, particularly those embedded in the
global technology supply chain, while countries outside it see
much less of a boost. Taiwan is riding booming semiconductor
demand, South Korea is beneﬁting from demand for high-
bandwidth memory, and the eﬀects travel upstream to the

suppliers of advanced chipmaking equipment, including the
Netherlands.

### Page 12

This illustrates how AI is reshaping both trade and investment
ﬂows. Spending on data centres and computing capacity in the US
drives demand for chips from Asia, which in turn requires
investment in semiconductor capacity and equipment from Europe,
Japan and the US. The result is an international AI investment
chain, with growth increasingly ﬂowing to the countries that hold
its critical links. It also explains a good part of the surge in the US
import bill: every data centre built in Texas shows up in Taiwan's

export statistics.
Trade policy is the second force reshaping the chain. Export controls
on advanced chips to China, tariﬀs and subsidy programmes such
as the US Chips Act (or direct government stakes in companies) are
pushing companies to build capacity closer to home or in friendly
countries. That makes the chain more resilient on paper, but also
more expensive – another reason why the deﬂationary promise of
AI may take longer to materialise.
The ﬂip side is concentration risk. If the chain snaps – through a

demand slump, an escalation of export controls or a Taiwan crisis –
the countries beneﬁting most today would feel it ﬁrst and hardest.
What does AI mean for labour markets, and
for how central banks read them?
So far, AI is reshaping who gets hired rather than how many are
employed – which makes it a real-time headache for central
banks.
Evidence on AI's labour market impact remains fragmented, and
the economy-wide eﬀect is still limited. If history is any guide, fears

of mass unemployment are overdone: with every major technology
of past centuries, growth was strong enough to create jobs
elsewhere, even as demand for some types of labour fell. But if
adoption is so fast that labour demand and supply can't adjust in
time, unemployment could rise temporarily.
The ﬁrst cracks are showing at the entry level. In the US, job growth
has remained positive but weaker in sectors with high AI exposure;
evidence from the eurozone is mixed. The Dallas Fed ﬁnds that

### Page 13

employment among young workers in AI-exposed occupations has
weakened, mainly reﬂecting lower hiring rather than more layoﬀs.
According to the Stanford Digital Economy Lab, employment
among 22-25-year-olds in highly exposed occupations was around
19% below where it would have been by June 2026 had it kept
pace with less-exposed occupations. In China, we see similar
pressures on entry-level work reﬂected in rising youth
unemployment. The Chinese government has planned training and

job replacement policies to help alleviate any negative eﬀects of AI
on employment. For developing Asia, particularly India and the
Philippines where outsourcing sectors are highly exposed to AI, the
evidence points to adaptation rather than decline. AI is changing
the nature of outsourced work, not reducing demand for it.
AI is not ﬁring people; it is quietly not hiring them. Still, none of this
points to mass displacement. Yet. And with ageing populations and

persistent labour shortages, the real question may be whether
shortages or AI turn out to be the bigger problem.
For central banks, AI is less of a short-run issue than you might
expect. Higher productivity growth combined with more muted job
growth could be the healthy sign of an economy absorbing a new
technology, and come with more moderate inﬂation. The tricky
part is reading it in real time. A soft payrolls print could signal either
a weakening economy or a more productive one – and the right

policy response would be very diﬀerent. Get it wrong, and a central
bank either cuts rates into a productivity boom or sits on its hands
as the economy weakens.
Who pays?
Is the boom still funded from cash ﬂows, or
are hyperscalers increasingly turning to
debt?
Still mostly from cash ﬂows – but the cushion is thinning and the
borrowing has begun.

### Page 14

The largest hyperscalers have so far paid for the AI build-out out of
their own pockets, which is the biggest diﬀerence from the dotcom
and railway booms. But capex is absorbing a rising share of
operating cash ﬂow, squeezing free cash ﬂow and leaving less room
for share buybacks. For companies that used to return cash to
shareholders at a record pace, that is a notable shift in priorities.
Borrowing is stepping up. Several hyperscalers, such as Amazon,

Alphabet, Meta, and Oracle have issued jumbo bond deals since
2025, with Oracle the most leveraged of the group. Equity markets
are being tapped too: several big tech ﬁrms announced plans to
raise equity in June. None of this is alarming on its own – these are
investment-grade companies with strong balance sheets – but it
marks a change of phase. Cash-funded booms are self-limiting:
they stop when the cash runs out. Debt-funded booms can run
further, and fall harder.

The key is to distinguish between the players. The cash-rich
hyperscalers can absorb a disappointment in AI returns; their core
businesses in advertising, cloud and software keep generating cash
regardless. The neoclouds and AI labs are a diﬀerent story. They
depend entirely on outside money – venture capital, private credit,
vendor ﬁnancing and strategic investors – and their business
models only work if revenue keeps growing spectacularly. They are

the most likely point of stress if ﬁnancing conditions tighten.
In between sits a growing grey zone of leases, joint ventures and
special purpose vehicles that keep debt oﬀ the hyperscalers' own
books.
The direction of travel is clear. The boom started as a self-funded
venture by the richest companies in the world. It is gradually
turning into a credit story.
Leases, SPVs, private credit: how opaque
has AI ﬁnancing become?
More opaque than the hyperscalers' balance sheets suggest – but

so far, the risk rests on some of the strongest borrowers in the
world.

### Page 15

A growing part of AI ﬁnancing is moving oﬀ balance sheet. Special
purpose vehicles and joint ventures, such as Meta's Hyperion data
centre ﬁnanced with Blue Owl, long-term leases and GPU-backed
loans allow companies to fund the build-out without the debt
appearing directly on their own books. Securitisation of data centre
assets through asset-backed securities and commercial mortgage-
backed securities is growing as well, bringing a wider pool of

investors into the game.
Leases are a particular blind spot. They are recognised as on-
balance-sheet commitments only once contracts are ﬁnal; until
then, the extent of incremental lease liabilities is uncertain. There is
also the question of who carries the risk if the tenant walks away
after the initial term. Although initial lease terms of 15 years are
not uncommon, these can still be shorter than the economic life of
the underlying infrastructure, leaving some residual risk around

renewal or re-leasing.
That said, risk has to be assessed contract by contract. The largest
technology ﬁrms, with very solid balance sheets, are well placed to
take on additional liabilities, and much of the oﬀ-balance-sheet
structure ultimately rests on their creditworthiness. The problem is
less hidden insolvency than hidden concentration: it is hard to see
how much of the system leans on the same handful of tenants.
Opacity also complicates the macro picture. When ﬁnancing is

spread across SPVs, private credit funds and securitisations, it
becomes harder for investors, regulators and central banks to see
where risks are concentrated – and harder to judge how a shock
would travel.
Looking ahead, capital may eventually become a major constraint
on the AI build-out, alongside permitting and access to power. The
more the industry relies on creative ﬁnancing, the more it depends
on investors' continued willingness to lend. Financial engineering

can prolong a boom, but it can't make the returns appear.
How exposed are banks, and is private
credit the shadow banking of this cycle?

### Page 16

Direct bank exposure looks modest; private credit is the obvious
weak spot – but contagion would run through markets rather than
bank failures.
Banks' direct exposure to AI looks limited. The hyperscalers fund
themselves mainly through cash ﬂow and bond markets rather
than bank loans. Indirect exposure is harder to gauge: banks lend to
private credit funds, data centre developers and construction ﬁrms.
Private credit is the obvious weak spot. Is it the shadow banking of

this cycle? Partly. It has less of the maturity mismatch that made
pre-2008 shadow banking so fragile: much of the capital is locked
up in closed-end funds, so investors can't run. But it shares other
features: opacity, infrequent valuation marks and leverage on
leverage, with funds borrowing from banks to lend to borrowers
that are themselves highly leveraged. And it is increasingly exposed
to AI through loans to neoclouds, GPU-backed ﬁnancing and data

centre developers.
The pressure point is the semi-liquid vehicles sold to retail and
wealth clients. They promise periodic liquidity on assets that are
anything but liquid. In a downturn, redemption requests could force
managers to impose gates, damaging conﬁdence across the asset
class. So far, these vehicles are not core to the AI build-out, but they
are where a shock could turn into a story.
If stress does materialise, we would expect contagion to run

through tighter ﬁnancial conditions and wider credit spreads across
the economy rather than through bank failures. That is less
dramatic than 2008, but not harmless: a repricing of credit risk
would raise funding costs far beyond tech, hitting the very non-tech
investment that is already struggling.
Banks may well escape an AI bust largely unscathed. The economy
they lend to may not.
Vendor ﬁnancing déjà vu: should we worry
when chipmakers fund their own

customers?
